{{Elections in Florida}}
The '''2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Florida''' will be held on November 3, 2026, to elect the 28 [[U.S. representative]]s from the [[U.S. state|State]] of [[Florida]], one from each of the state's [[Florida's congressional districts|congressional districts]]. The elections will coincide with [[2026 United States House of Representatives elections|other elections]] to the House of Representatives, [[2026 United States Senate elections|elections]] to the [[United States Senate]], and various [[2026 United States elections|state and local elections]]. Primary elections took place on August 18, 2026.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|date=May 9, 2025 |title=2026 State Primary Election Dates |url=https://www.ncsl.org/elections-and-campaigns/2026-state-primary-election-dates|publisher=NCSL |access-date=August 5, 2025}}</ref>

==Redistricting==
{{main|2026 Florida redistricting}}
[[File:Florida Congressional Districts, 120th Congress (Plan EOGPCRP2026).svg|thumb|upright=1.25|Map of [[Florida's congressional districts]] as passed by the [[Florida Legislature]] on April 29, 2026, which will be in use at the 2026 elections pending legal challenges<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|last=Fineout |first=Gary |date=May 15, 2026 |title=Judge weighs effort to block new Florida congressional map |url=https://www.politico.com/news/2026/05/15/florida-redistricting-2026-court-case-00923923 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260516010802/https://www.politico.com/news/2026/05/15/florida-redistricting-2026-court-case-00923923 |archive-date=May 16, 2026 |access-date=May 16, 2026 |work=[[Politico]]}}</ref><br/>{{maplink|from=Florida's 1st congressional district (2027–).map|from2=Florida's 2nd congressional district (2023–).map|from3=Florida's 3rd congressional district 28th congressional district (2027–).map|text=Interactive map version}}]]

On April 29, 2026, the Florida House of Representatives and State Senate passed [[2026 Florida redistricting|a new congressional district map]] proposed by Governor Ron DeSantis during a special legislative session. The map, which takes effect for the 2026 elections, is expected to result in a 24–4 Republican–Democratic split among the state's 28 congressional districts.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|first=Matt |last=Dixon |date=April 29, 2026 |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/florida-legislature-passes-redistricting-plan-creating-four-additional-rcna342656 |title=Florida Legislature Passes Redistricting Plan Creating Four Additional GOP-Leaning House Seats |publisher=NBC News |access-date=April 30, 2026}}</ref><ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|first=Bill |last=Barrow |date=April 29, 2026 |url=https://apnews.com/article/florida-ron-desantis-donald-trump-redistricting-13e14f95a8d2b6afbc7e3e698f5f9256 |title=Florida Legislature Approves New Congressional Map Intended to Boost Republicans in Midterms |publisher=Associated Press |access-date=May 2, 2026}}</ref> Governor DeSantis signed the redistricting map into law on May 4. The map is being challenged in court on the basis that it violates the [[Fair Districts Amendment]] to the state constitution that passed in [[2010 Florida Amendment 6|2010]], which prohibits an explicitly partisan map.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|first=Romy |last=Ellenbogen |date=May 4, 2026 |url=https://www.tampabay.com/news/florida-politics/elections/2026/05/04/florida-redistricting-desantis-maps-gop-republican-congress-trump/ |title=DeSantis Signs Florida Redistricting Map, Drawing Quick Legal Challenge |work=Tampa Bay Times |access-date=May 5, 2026}}</ref> On May 26, a judge ruled that the new map will stay in effect for the 2026 elections.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|first=Romy |last=Ellenbogen |date=May 26, 2026 |url=https://www.miamiherald.com/news/politics-government/state-politics/article315898882.html |title=Effort to Stop Florida's New, GOP-Friendly Congressional Map Blocked by Judge |work=Miami Herald |access-date=May 28, 2026}}</ref>

The incumbent is Republican [[Neal Dunn]], who was re-elected with 61.6% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT">{{#invoke:cite|web|url=https://www.cookpolitical.com/vote-tracker/2024/house|title=2024 House Vote Tracker|access-date=December 2, 2024 |website=[[The Cook Political Report with Amy Walter|Cook Political Report]]}}</ref> On January 13, 2026, Dunn announced that he would not run for re-election.<ref name="Dunn"/>

==District 6==
{{see also|Florida's 6th congressional district}}
{{Infobox election
| election_name = 2026 Florida's 6th congressional district election
| country = Florida
| type = presidential

| party1 = Republican Party (United States)

| nominee2 = Eric Yonce
| party2 = Democratic Party (United States)
| popular_vote2 =

[[File:Florida's 6th congressional district (since 2027) (new version).svg|thumb|270px|Florida's 6th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections<br/>{{maplink|from=Florida's 6th congressional district (2023–).map|text=Interactive map version}}]]

Incumbent Republican [[Randy Fine]] won the primary election by a large margin.<ref name="wp">{{#invoke:cite|web|last=Beauchamp |first=Zack |date=2026-08-19 |title=The worst primary in America just ended |url=https://www.vox.com/politics/499767/randy-fine-dan-bilzerian-florida-6-district |access-date=2026-08-19 |website=Vox |language=en-US}}</ref> Fine assumed office on April 2, 2025, after winning the [[2025 Florida's 6th congressional district special election|special election]] that occurred after [[Mike Waltz]] resigned to become [[U.S. National Security Advisor|national security advisor]]. He was elected with 56.6% of the vote. Fine was endorsed for re-election by President [[Donald Trump]].<ref name="Fine Vote">{{#invoke:cite|news|url=https://floridapolitics.com/archives/800922-cory-mills-to-face-ryan-elijah-in-gop-primary-as-randy-fine-draws-republican-challenge-from-dan-bilzerian/|title=Cory Mills to face Ryan Elijah in GOP Primary, as Randy Fine draws Republican challenge from Dan Bilzerian|last=Ogles|first=Jacob|date=June 9, 2026|website=[[Florida Politics]]}}</ref>

Fine's anti-Palestinian, Islamophobic, and racist comments on [[X (social network)|X]] regarding the [[Gaza war]] have sparked controversy and backlash, including condemnation from the [[American Jewish Committee]].<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|last=Rimmer|first=Morgan|title=House Democratic leaders call on GOP congressman to apologize over 'unhinged, racist and Islamophobic' attack on Ilhan Omar|url=https://www.cnn.com/2025/07/09/politics/ilhan-omar-randy-fine-house-democrats|publisher=[[CNN]]|date=July 9, 2025}}</ref><ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|url=https://www.reuters.com/world/us/us-rights-advocates-democrats-condemn-lawmaker-randy-fines-comments-racist-2025-07-10/|title=US rights advocates, Democrats condemn lawmaker Randy Fine's comments as racist|publisher=[[Reuters]]|date=July 9, 2025|last=Singh|first=Kanishka}}</ref><ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|last=Olmstead |first=Edith |date=July 28, 2025 |title=Not Even AIPAC Wants to Be Associated With This GOP Rep Anymore |url=https://newrepublic.com/post/198516/aipac-drops-gop-representative-starving-kids-gaza |access-date=August 17, 2025 |website=[[The New Republic]] |language=en-US}}</ref>  His challengers were viewed as likely to split the vote against him.<ref name="Fine Vote"/>

Gambaro has attacked Fine for his [[anti-Palestinian racism]] and called him a "security risk", while Baker has also criticized Fine as [[Israel first|Israel First]].<ref name="Fine Vote"/> Conversely, Bilzerian engaged in [[antisemitic]] rhetoric, which has included calling Fine a "fat Jew"; Bilzerian defended his comments by saying that Fine has made worse comments, pointing to when Fine stated that he preferred dogs to [[Muslims]].<ref name="Fine Vote"/> Bilzerian released a video referring to Fine as a "Satanic whore" and "[[Shylock]]", while praising [[Adolf Hitler]]<ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|last=Harper|first=Mark|title=Antisemitic ad attacks Randy Fine, boosts Hitler's opinions|url=https://www.news-journalonline.com/story/news/politics/elections/congressional/2026/08/16/republican-leaders-condemn-bilzerians-antisemitism/91328049007/|access-date=2026-08-17|website=Daytona Beach News-Journal Online|language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|last=Zimmermann|first=David|date=2026-08-17|title=Randy Fine's primary opponent condemned for antisemitic ad|url=https://www.washingtonexaminer.com/news/campaigns/congressional/4689612/dan-bilzerian-antisemitic-ad-florida-randy-fine/|access-date=2026-08-17|website=Washington Examiner|language=en-US}}</ref> and envisioning a repetition of the [[The Holocaust|Holocaust]].<ref name="wp"/> By the final days of the campaign, Bilzerian's rhetoric had been denounced by multiple Republican and Democratic congressmen and the American Jewish Committee.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|last=Mondeaux |first=Cami |date=2026-08-17 |title=Republicans and Democrats decry antisemitic attacks in tense Florida GOP primary |url=https://www.deseret.com/politics/2026/08/17/republicans-decry-antisemitic-ad-florida-primary/ |access-date=2026-08-19 |website=Deseret News |language=en}}</ref>

Fine criticized Bilzerian for holding Armenian citizenship,<ref name="wp"/> and late in the primary campaign, referred Bilzerian to the FBI for alleged campaign finance irregularities.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|last=Zagelbaum |first=Rikki |date=2026-08-18 |title=Rep. Fine refers GOP opponent to FBI over $1 million in allegedly unreported campaign expenditures |url=https://www.clevelandjewishnews.com/jns/rep-fine-refers-gop-opponent-to-fbi-over-1-million-in-allegedly-unreported-campaign-expenditures/article_226ee034-a657-5e59-bb4d-f85a7755aeb4.html |access-date=2026-08-19 |website=Cleveland Jewish News |language=en}}</ref> On the day of the Republican primary, a video of Fine was posted online of him illegally looking inside of a voter's mailbox. According to ''[[CBS]]'', Fine's actions constituted [[Mail and wire fraud#Mail fraud|mail fraud]] and violated federal law; however, the homeowner declined to press charges against Fine.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|url=https://cbs12.com/news/local/us-rep-randy-fine-caught-snooping-inside-homes-mailbox-tmz-video-no-federal-charges-ring-camera-video-front-porch-ormond-beach-postal-service-mail-gop-primary-battle|title=US Rep. Randy Fine caught snooping inside home's mailbox|last=Comas|first=Martin E.|work=CBS 12|date=19 August 2026}}</ref>

[[File:Florida's 10th congressional district (since 2027) (new version).svg|thumb|270px|Florida's 10th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections<br/>{{maplink|from=Florida's 10th congressional district (2027–).map|text=Interactive map version}}]]
The incumbent is Democrat [[Maxwell Frost]], who was re-elected with 62.4% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT"/> Frost is the only candidate after all opponents failed to qualify, resulting in the primary and general elections being cancelled under Florida law.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|last1=Ogles |first1=Jacob |title=Maxwell Frost secures another term in Congress unopposed |url=https://floridapolitics.com/archives/801710-maxwell-frost-effectively-secures-another-term-in-congress/ |website=[[Florida Politics]] - Campaigns & Elections. Lobbying & Government |access-date=June 13, 2026 |date=June 13, 2026}}</ref>

The incumbent is Republican [[Byron Donalds]], who was re-elected with 66.3% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT"/> Donalds is retiring to [[2026 Florida gubernatorial election|run for governor]].<ref name="Donalds">{{#invoke:cite|news|last1=Leonard |first1=Kimberly |last2=Matat |first2=Stephany |title=Rep. Byron Donalds, backed by Trump, says he's running for Florida governor|url=https://apnews.com/article/byron-donalds-florida-governor-elections-2026-trump-5884b17305d6ef64408934dbdd65a210 |access-date=February 25, 2025 |publisher=[[Associated Press]] |date=February 25, 2025 |language=en}}</ref>

The Republican primary featured two former members of Congress from other states, [[Madison Cawthorn]] ([[North Carolina's 11th congressional district|NC-11]])<ref name="mc"/> and [[Chris Collins (New York politician)|Chris Collins]] ([[New York's 27th congressional district|NY-27]]).<ref name="cc"/> Catalina Lauf and Jim Oberweis had previously faced each other in the 2020 Republican primary in Illinois' 14th congressional district.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|first=Steve |last=Contorno |date=August 7, 2026 |url=https://www.cnn.com/2026/08/07/politics/florida-primary-madison-cawthorn-trump |title=A 'Clown Show' of Carpetbaggers and Characters Has Upended a Republican Primary in Trump's Backyard |work=CNN |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260807141103/https://www.cnn.com/2026/08/07/politics/florida-primary-madison-cawthorn-trump |archive-date=August 7, 2026 |url-status=live |access-date=August 19, 2026}}</ref>

This seat is vacant following the resignation of Democrat [[Sheila Cherfilus-McCormick]] on April 21, 2026,<ref name="HPVT"/> who was re-elected in an uncontested race in 2024.

==District 23==
{{see also|Florida's 23rd congressional district}}
{{Infobox election
| election_name = 2026 Florida's 23rd congressional district election
| country = Florida
| type = presidential
| ongoing = yes
| previous_election = 2024 United States House of Representatives elections in Florida#District 23

| party1 = Democratic Party (United States)
| popular_vote1 = 
| percentage1 =

[[File:Florida's 23rd congressional district (since 2027) (new version).svg|thumb|270px|Florida's 23rd congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections<br/>{{maplink|from=Florida's 23rd congressional district (2027–).map|text=Interactive map version}}]]
The 23rd district covers parts of [[Broward County, Florida|Broward County]] and southern [[Palm Beach County, Florida|Palm Beach County]], including the cities of [[Boca Raton, Florida|Boca Raton]], [[Coral Springs, Florida|Coral Springs]], and most of [[Deerfield Beach, Florida|Deerfield Beach]] and [[Fort Lauderdale, Florida|Fort Lauderdale]]. The incumbent is Democrat [[Jared Moskowitz]], who was re-elected with 52.4% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT"/>

====Nominee====
*[[Lois Frankel]], incumbent U.S. representative from the 22nd district ''(previously ran in the 22nd district)''<ref name="FrankelSwitch"/>

====Eliminated in primary====
*Victoria Doyle, attorney ''(previously ran in the 22nd district)''<ref name="sr"/>
*Mark Piper<ref name="CanList"/>

====Withdrawn====
*Oliver Larkin, digital strategist and union organizer ''(running in the 25th district)''<ref name="Larkin25">{{#invoke:cite|web|last1=Ogles |first1=Jacob |title=Redistricting shuffle: Oliver Larkin is shifting candidacy to CD 25, if the new Florida map holds |url=https://floridapolitics.com/archives/796449-redistricting-shuffle-oliver-larkin-is-shifting-candidacy-to-cd-25-if-the-new-florida-map-holds/ |website=[[Florida Politics]] |access-date=May 14, 2026 |date=May 14, 2026}}</ref>
*[[Jared Moskowitz]], incumbent U.S. representative ''(running in the 25th district)''<ref name="Moskowitz 25th"/>

[[File:Florida's 24th congressional district (since 2027) (new version).svg|thumb|270px|Florida's 24th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections<br/>{{maplink|from=Florida's 24th congressional district (2027–).map|text=Interactive map version}}]]
The incumbent is Democrat [[Frederica Wilson]], who was re-elected with 68.2% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT"/> She announced in May 2026 that she would not seek another term.

[[File:Florida's 25th congressional district (since 2027) (new version).svg|thumb|270px|Florida's 25th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections<br/>{{maplink|from=Florida's 25th congressional district (2027–).map|text=Interactive map version}}]]
The incumbent is Democrat [[Debbie Wasserman Schultz]], who was re-elected with 54.5% of the vote in 2024.<ref name="HPVT"/>

The election was one of the most expensive congressional races in the 2026 elections, with over $42.2 million in spending.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Mutnick|first=Ally|date=October 6, 2026|title=The blockbuster House races|url=https://punchbowl.news/article/campaigns/blockbuster-house-races/|access-date=2026-10-15|website=Punchbowl News|language=en-US}}</ref>

===Democratic primary===
====Nominee====
*Phil Ehr, nonprofit executive, former Navy commander, nominee for this district in [[2024 United States House of Representatives elections in Florida#District 28|2024]], nominee for the 1st district in [[2020 United States House of Representatives elections in Florida#District 1|2020]] and candidate in [[2018 United States House of Representatives elections in Florida#District 1|2018]]<ref name="dozens">{{#invoke:cite|news|last1=Solender |first1=Andrew |title=Scoop: Dozens of Dem candidates won't commit to backing Jeffries |url=https://www.axios.com/2025/10/16/hakeem-jeffries-speaker-leader-democrats-primary |access-date=October 16, 2025 |date=October 16, 2025 |website=[[Axios (website)|Axios]] |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251016075142/https://www.axios.com/2025/10/16/hakeem-jeffries-speaker-leader-democrats-primary |archive-date=October 16, 2025}}</ref>

====Withdrawn====
